[^627]: Psalm 112:2 (LXX), sung thrice as the transition from the Artoklasia to the final blessing and patristic reading.

[^628]: Psalm 33 (LXX 33:1–11): "I will bless the Lord at all times; His praise shall continually be in my mouth..." In the ancient Studite and Sabbaitic typika, this psalm was sung while the blessed bread and wine from the Artoklasia were distributed to the brethren in the narthex to sustain them during the prolonged vigil.

[^629]: In early modern Ruthenian liturgical editions, "the Great Doors" (*вели́кїѧ вра́та*) denotes the central Holy Doors (Royal Doors) of the iconostasis.

[^630]: The rubric prescribes the ancient Vigil custom of a patristic or Scriptural instructional reading (*съсѣда́нїе* / kathisma reading) inserted between Vespers and Matins, taken either from the Acts of the Apostles (*ѿ а҆́пла*), the Epistles of Saint Paul (*пꙋ́л*), or the Life of the Saint (*жи́тїе ст҃го*).

[^631]: This rubric governs Great Vespers when celebrated as an independent evening service without an All-Night Vigil, wherein the blessing of loaves (*лития / артокласия*) is omitted and the service proceeds immediately to the final dismissal.

[^632]: Here again *ве[ли́каѧ] вра́та* refers to the central Holy Doors of the iconostasis, which are opened by the deacon for the concluding blessing.

[^633]: The ancient dismissal dialogue begins with the blessing *Сы́й бл҃венъ и҆ препросла́вленъ х҃съ б҃ъ на́шъ* ("Blessed and supreme in glory is Christ our God"), corresponding directly to the Byzantine Greek liturgical response Ὁ ὢν εὐλογητὸς Χριστὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν.

[^634]: The rubric strictly specifies that at the dismissal on Saturday evening (i.e. at Great Vespers preceding Sunday), the phrase "Who rose from the dead" (*Въскр҃съ и҆з̾ мр҃твы́хъ*) is omitted from the initial phrase of the dismissal formula, as the full Resurrectional commemoration properly belongs to the Sunday morning dismissals.

[^635]: This marks the beginning of the Order of the Sacerdotal Litany and Prayer of Intercession for Deliverance from Pestilence and Calamity, appointed to be said on Sundays following the Midnight Office Trinity Canon (*по кано́нѣ трⷪ҇ичнѣ*), as well as daily at the conclusion of Compline and the Midnight Office.

[^636]: The priest performs this office standing outside the sanctuary before the closed Holy Doors (*прѣд вели́кыми враты̀*), wearing the epitrachelion over his cassock.

[^637]: The Slavonic text reads *въсѧ́комоу гра́дꙋ и҆ за́нѣ*, where *за́нѣ* represents an Early Modern Ruthenian/Slavonic dialectal form for *странѣ̀* / *стра́нѣ* (countryside, province, territory, or district).

[^638]: A seasonal or pastoral rubric prescribing an additional clause during prolonged droughts (*бездо́жїе*), imploring the Lord to send seasonable rains for the fertility of the earth.

[^639]: The choir responds to the supplicatory litany with forty repetitions of "Lord, have mercy" (*Г҃и поми́лꙋй , м҃*).

[^640]: The Sunday dismissal incipit *Въскр҃съ и҆з̾ мр҃твы́хъ* ("He that rose from the dead..."), printed here as the opening formula concluding the Sunday post-Compline/Midnight Office intercessory prayers.

[^641]: The rubrical manual act prescribes that the priest, having removed his epitrachelion (*ѡ҆тлага́е є҆пітрахи́ль*), turns toward the nave to face the brethren and congregation (*ѡ҆бра́щьсѧ къ наро́дѡмъ*) and makes a low bow or prostration (*мета́нїе* / metania) before asking mutual forgiveness (*прощение*).

[^642]: The traditional monastic formula of mutual forgiveness (*Бл҃гослови́те мѧ̀ ѻ҃тцы ст҃і́и и҆ прости́те мѧ̀ грѣ́шнаго*), recited by the celebrant at the conclusion of Compline and the Midnight Office.

[^643]: The reciprocal mutual forgiveness exchanged quietly (*ле́гко*) among all the monastics or faithful in choir (*Та́кожде и҆ въси къ себѣ̀ то́же глю́тъ*), following the ancient Sabbaitic and Studite monastic custom.

[^644]: The opening intercessory petition for the civil authorities: *Помо́лимсѧ ѡ҆ бл҃гоꙋти́выхъ и҆ бг҃омъ храни́мыхъ цр҃е́хъ и҆ кнѧзѣ́ на́ши*. The word *бл҃гоꙋти́выхъ* is a typographical variant in the Striatyn edition for *бл҃гочести́выхъ* ("pious" or "devout").

[^645]: Throughout this intercessory litany, the choir responds to each successive invocation with the formula: "May God forgive and have mercy on them" (*Бг҃ъ да прости́тъ и҆ поми́лꙋетъ и҆́хъ* / *Бг҃ъ да прости́ и҆ поми́лꙋетъ ѧ҃*), characteristic of the Slavic post-Compline and Midnight Office intercessions.

[^646]: The term *кти́торы* (from Byzantine Greek κτήτωρ) designates the founders, benefactors, and legal patrons of the monastery or church.

[^647]: The Slavonic text reads *Ѡ҆ недꙋ́гꙋющи и҆ зажа́щи*, where *зажа́щи* represents those held in restraint, custody, or captivity (derived from *зажати* / *задержати*, parallel to *плѣне́нныѧ* / αἰχμαλώτων).

[^648]: The rubric directs that on Sundays following the intercessions, the reader recites the title/incipit of the Epistle reading (*написа́нїе а҆пл҃а*), introducing an instructional patristic or Scriptural reading before the subsequent office.

[^649]: The priest imparts the traditional blessing (*За мл҃твъ ст҃ы́хъ ѡ҃цъ на́шихъ...*) authorizing the reader to begin the appointed instructional reading.

[^650]: If it be an ordinary ferial day (*а҆́ще нѣ́сть недѣ́лѧ , нѝ нарочи́тъ ст҃ы́й*), the service proceeds immediately to the solemn commemoration of the departed (*Помѧне́мъ правосла́вныа...*).

[^651]: The solemn liturgical singing of "Eternal memory" (*Вѣ́чнаѧ и҆́мъ па́мѧ*) concluding the commemorations of departed rulers, hierarchs, benefactors, and brethren.

[^652]: A woodcut headpiece and title in ornate Cyrillic vyaz: *Послѣдова́нїе всенощное* ("The Order of the All-Night Vigil"). In early modern Ruthenian Liturgika, this section details the priestly and diaconal prayers, litanies, and ceremonial movements for Matins (*На ᲂу҆́трени*) when celebrated as part of the All-Night Vigil.

[^653]: The curtain of the Royal Doors (*катапета́зма* / καταπέτασма) is opened before the opening doxology, while the Holy Doors themselves may remain closed until the Polyeleos or Hexapsalmos according to Typikon rubrics.

[^654]: Psalm 19 (LXX 19:2–10; Masoretic 20:1–9): "The Lord give ear unto thee in the day of affliction..." The first of the two Royal Psalms recited at the beginning of the introductory Royal Office of Matins.

[^655]: Psalm 20 (LXX 20:2–14; Masoretic 21:1–13): "The king shall be glad in Thy strength, O Lord..." The second Royal Psalm.

[^656]: The Troparion of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross (*Спаси, Господи, люди Твоя...*), appointed as the first troparion of the Royal Office of Matins.

[^657]: The Kontakion of the Holy Cross (*Вознесыйся на Крест волею...*), sung after "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit."

[^658]: The Theotokion of the Royal Office (*Предстательство страшное и непостыдное...*), sung after "Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages."

[^659]: The priest proclaims the solemn initial doxology of the All-Night Vigil Matins (*Сла́ва ст҃ѣ́й є҆диносꙋ́щнѣй и҆ животворѧ́щой и҆ нераздѣли́мѣй трⷪ҇ици...*) with a loud voice (*съ гла́сомъ*), making the sign of the cross with the censer before the Holy Table.

[^660]: The Six Psalms (Hexapsalmos: Psalms 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, and 142 LXX), beginning with the Angelic Hymn from Luke 2:14: "Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will toward men."

[^661]: Psalm 87 (LXX 87:2; Masoretic 88:1): "O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before Thee." During the second half of the Hexapsalmos, beginning at this psalm, the priest recites the twelve Morning Prayers.

[^662]: The Striatyn Liturgikon specifies that the priest says the twelve Morning Prayers secretly within the altar (*гле́тъ і҆ере́й млт҃вы ᲂу҆́трнѣѧ , въ ѡ҆лтарѝ*), whereas post-Nikonian rubrics generally direct the priest to recite them bareheaded before the closed Royal Doors in the nave.

[^663]: Psalm 117:27 (LXX 117:27; Masoretic 118:27): "God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us; blessed is he that comes in the Name of the Lord."

[^664]: The Seventeenth Kathisma of the Psalter (Psalm 118 LXX; Masoretic 119), known as the *Непоро́чны* (Amomos / "The Blameless"), traditionally sung at Sunday Vigil Matins prior to the Evlogitaria.

[^665]: The Resurrectional Troparia (*Тропари по Непорочнах* / Evlogitaria of the Resurrection), beginning with the refrain *Благословен еси, Господи, научи мя оправданием Твоим* and the first troparion *Ангельский собор удивися* ("The assembly of the Angels was amazed...").

[^666]: Typographical pagination error: The Striatyn printer repeated the Cyrillic numeral `у҃па` (481) on fol. 247v (recte p. 482), causing the printed pagination to be one number lower than the true physical page count through the end of the signature.

[^667]: The Polyeleos (*Многомилостиво*), consisting of Psalms 134 and 135 (LXX), sung at festal Matins and followed by the Little Litany and the priestly exclamation *Яко святися и прославися...*

[^668]: The Hymns of Degrees (Anabathmoi / *Антифоны степенны*), based on the Gradual Psalms (Psalms 119–133 LXX), followed by the Matins Prokeimenon.

[^669]: Psalm 150:6 and 150:1 (LXX): "Let every breath praise the Lord" and "Praise God in His saints," sung as the dialogue introducing the Matins Gospel reading.

[^670]: The rubric notes that after the Gospel, the hymn "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." or the post-Gospel sticheron is not sung at this point (*По є҆ѵлїи не стрⷪ҇а*), proceeding directly to the diaconal intercession.

[^671]: The solemn prayer of intercession: *Спаси, Боже, люди Твоя, и благослови достояние Твое...* ("O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."), with twelve repetitions of "Lord, have mercy" (*Г҃и поми́лꙋй , в҃і*).

[^672]: The Matins Canon, consisting of nine odes (with Ode 2 traditionally omitted except during Great Lent). The deacon performs a full censing of the altar and church at Ode 1 and Ode 9.

[^673]: The censing of the Holy Table, the sanctuary, and the congregation at the Ninth Ode, accompanying the Magnificat (*Песнь Богородицы* / *Честнейшую херувим*).

[^674]: The Sunday Exapostilarion hymn: *Свят Господь Бог наш* ("Holy is the Lord our God," Psalm 98:5, 9 LXX), proclaimed by the deacon on Sundays before the Photagogicon / Exapostilarion.

[^675]: The Praises (Lauds / *Хвалитны*), Psalms 148, 149, and 150 (LXX), with the festal Stichera on the Praises appointed by the Typikon.

[^676]: The ceremonial exit of the priest and deacon through the Royal Doors to stand before the principal icons of the iconostasis (the priest before the icon of the Mother of God, and the deacon before the icon of Christ) during the singing of the Great Doxology.

[^677]: The Great Doxology (*Славословие Великое*), beginning *Слава в вышних Богу...* ("Glory to God in the highest...").

[^678]: The Augmented Litany (*Ектения сугубая*), repeating the same sequence of civic and ecclesiastical intercessions established at Great Vespers (see fol. 231v–233v / pp. 450–454).

[^679]: The Morning Litany of Supplication (*Ектения просительная*), beginning *Исполним утреннюю молитву нашу Господеви* ("Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord").

[^680]: The Prayer at the Bowing of Heads at Matins (*Молитва главопреклонения*), beginning *Господи святый, живый в вышних...* ("O Holy Lord, Who dwellest on high..."), recited secretly (*тайно*) by the priest facing the Holy Table while the faithful bow their heads.

[^681]: The conclusion of the Prayer at the Bowing of Heads at Sunday All-Night Vigil Matins: *при́зри рꙋко́ю тво́ею неви́димо, ѿ ст҃го жили́ща твое́гѡ...* ("look down invisibly with Thy hand from Thy holy habitation..."). The prayer beseeches God's merciful pardon for sins committed voluntarily or involuntarily and asks for earthly and heavenly blessings.

[^682]: The priestly exclamation concluding the Prayer at the Bowing of Heads: *Ꙗ҆́кѡ твоѐ є҆́, є҆́же ми́ловати и҆ сп҃сти́ ны бж҃е на́шъ...* ("For Thine it is to have mercy and to save us, O our God...").

[^683]: The blessing formula pronounced by the celebrant at the dismissal dialogue of Matins: *Сы́й бл҃гослове́нъ и҆ препросла́вленъ хс҃ъ бг҃ъ на́шъ* ("Blessed and praised is He Who is, Christ our God..."), corresponding to the Byzantine *Ὁ ὢν εὐλογητὸς Χριστὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*.

[^684]: The Megalynarion hymn to the Mother of God: "More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim..." (*Честнѣйшую херувим*), sung by the choir prior to the final dismissal dialogue.

[^685]: The rubric introduces the final liturgical dismissal (*ѿпꙋ́стъ* / apolysis) of Sunday Matins when celebrated as part of the All-Night Vigil.

[^686]: The initial festive clause of the Sunday dismissal: *Възкре́съ и҆з̾ ме́ртвыхъ* ("May He Who rose from the dead..."), confessing the Lord's bodily Resurrection.

[^687]: The rubric directs the priest to insert the name of the celebrated saint (*ст҃ы́й наро́читъ*) commemorated on that particular calendar day into the dismissal formula before the general commemoration of all saints.

[^688]: The rubric specifies that if the First Hour is celebrated immediately following Matins (as is customary in parish and monastic practice), this second, more complete dismissal formula is recited at the conclusion of the First Hour.

[^689]: The complete dismissal formula recited at the conclusion of the First Hour on Sundays, invoking the Mother of God, the venerable God-bearing monastic fathers (*прпⷣбныхъ и҆ бг҃оно́сныхъ ѻ҃ц҃ъ на́шихъ*), and all saints.

[^690]: An ornate Renaissance woodcut headpiece depicting an eagle bearing an open tablet inscribed with the Holy Trinity, introducing the rubrics and prayers for Daily Vespers and Matins (*Ѡ҆ побъседне́внои вече́рни, и҆ ᲂу҆́трзни*).

[^691]: In the Byzantine Rite, simple daily services without a celebrated saint or feast omit the solemn Entrance (*вход*) with the censer or Gospel at Vespers.

[^692]: If the commemorated saint has no Great Doxology appointed in the Menaion or Typikon, the service follows the simple daily rite (ferial Vespers and ferial Matins).

[^693]: The rubric directs that at simple Daily Vespers, the opening blessing and prayers are performed by the priest standing directly outside the closed Royal Doors in the nave (*прѣ вели́кыми враты̀*), rather than inside the holy sanctuary.

[^694]: The priest recites the seven secret Lamp-lighting Prayers (*молитвы светильничные*) before the Royal Doors during the recitation of the Kathisma or opening psalm.

[^695]: The Little Litany (*мала́ дїа́конства* / Little Synapte) is recited by the priest or deacon inside the altar before the holy table, followed by its appointed exclamation.

[^696]: The exclamation concluding the Little Litany after the Kathisma at Daily Vespers: *Ꙗ҆́ко твоѧ̀ дръжа́ва* ("For Thine is the dominion, and Thine is the kingdom...").

[^697]: The rubric prescribes that a full censing of the entire temple (*кади́ти подоба́етъ всего́*) takes place at "Lord, I have cried" on eves of feast days, on feasts, and on ordinary ferial days alike.

[^698]: The text provides an explicit internal cross-reference to the Litany of Supplication (*И҆спо́лнимъ вече́рнюю моли́тву на́шу Го́сподеви*) and its secret prayers printed earlier in the codex at fol. 228r–229v (pp. 443–446) during Great Vespers.

[^699]: Following the singing of the Aposticha stichera (*стїховнѣ ст҃ры*) and the dismissal troparion, the priest stands before the Royal Doors to proclaim the Augmented Litany (*Поми́луй на́съ, Бо́же...*).

[^700]: The Striatyn Liturgikon preserves distinct liturgical petitions for monastic communities (*а҆́ще є҆́сть въ монастыри рци́ сїе*) and parish churches (*а҆́ще є҆́сть не въ монастыри*), reflecting the dual pastoral and cenobitic usage of the Balaban printing press.

[^701]: The conclusion and daily dismissal of simple ferial Vespers, preceded by the dialogue "Wisdom!", "Blessed and praised is He Who is...", and "More honorable than the Cherubim."

[^702]: Rubrics governing the celebration of Daily Matins (*Оᲊ́ТРЬНИ*), beginning with the censing of the sanctuary and nave before the Royal Office and Hexapsalmos.

[^703]: The rubric appoints a full censing of the church prior to the recitation of the Six Psalms (Hexapsalmos: Psalms 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, and 142 LXX).

[^704]: The priest recites the abbreviated Augmented Litany of the Royal Office inside the altar while holding the censer (*въ ѻ҃лтари, съ кади́лницею*), praying for the sovereign monarch and the monastic brotherhood.

[^705]: The solemn opening exclamation of Matins: *Сла́ва ст҃ѣ́й є҆диносоу́щнѣй и҆ животворѧ́щей и҆ нераздѣли́мѣй Тро́ицѣ...* ("Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity...").

[^706]: At Daily Matins, the celebrant recites the Morning Prayers and litany petitions stationed in the nave before the Royal Doors (*прѣ вели́кыми враты́*), symbolizing the exile of Adam outside the gates of Paradise.

[^707]: A distinct liturgical rubric of the 1604 Striatyn edition: the diaconal petitions after the Fourth Ode of the Matins Canon are recited inside the altar rather than in the nave.

[^708]: The celebrant performs a censing of the sanctuary and church during the Fourth Ode of the Canon at daily services.

[^709]: Following the recitation of the Lesser Doxology ("Glory to God in the highest," read rather than sung on ferial days), the priest proclaims the Morning Litany of Supplication before the Royal Doors.

[^710]: Internal cross-reference to the secret Prayer at the Bowing of Heads at Matins (*Господи святый, живый в вышних...*), printed earlier at fol. 250r (p. 484 [recte 485]).

[^711]: Psalm 91:2 LXX: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord, and to sing praises unto Thy Name, O Most High," recited twice at Daily Matins prior to the Trisagion Prayers.

[^712]: The final Augmented Litany of Daily Matins recited by the celebrant before the Royal Doors following the Trisagion and dismissal troparion.

[^713]: In the daily monastic cursus, the First Hour is appended immediately to the conclusion of Matins without an intervening dismissal.

[^714]: A specialized rubrical directive clarifying the proper usage of the two alternative blessing formulas: "God be bountiful unto us and bless us" (*Богъ да ущедрит ны*) versus "Through the prayers of our holy fathers" (*За молитвъ святыхъ отецъ нашихъ*).

[^715]: The Prayer of the Hours attributed to Saint Basil the Great or Saint Dorotheus: "Thou Who at every time and at every hour, in heaven and on earth, art worshipped and glorified, O Christ God..." (*Иже на всякое время и на всякий час...*).

[^716]: Psalm 66:2 LXX: *Бо́гъ да ᲂу҆ще́дритъ ны́, и҆ бл҃гослови́тъ ны́, просвѣти́тъ лицѐ своѐ на ны́, и҆ поми́лꙋетъ ны́* ("God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us"), appointed as the celebrant's blessing at the Midnight Office and the First Hour.

[^717]: The traditional monastic blessing formula: *За мл҃твъ ст҃ы́хъ ѻ҃цъ на́шихъ, г҃и і҆с҃ хс҃е бж҃е на́шъ, поми́лꙋй на́съ* ("Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us"), appointed at the Third and Sixth Hours.

[^718]: When the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours are read together in an aggregated liturgical block (*въ коу́пѣ часо́ве пою́тсѧ*), the blessing "Through the prayers..." is used at the Ninth Hour as well.

[^719]: The concluding sequence of the aggregated Hours: Psalm 33:2 LXX ("I will bless the Lord at all times"), "It is truly meet," and "More honorable than the Cherubim."

[^720]: The blessing formula "God be bountiful unto us" is also recited by the priest at the preparatory beginning of the Divine Liturgy before the opening exclamation.

[^721]: If the Ninth Hour is chanted separately as the immediate prelude to Vespers (rather than aggregated with the Third and Sixth Hours), the blessing "God be bountiful unto us" is said.

[^722]: At Great Compline (celebrated during Great Lent), the service is divided into three distinct sections; after the first section (and its forty repetitions of "Lord, have mercy"), the priest says "Through the prayers of our holy fathers."

[^723]: Following the second section of Great Compline, the priest recites the prayer of Manasseh or the blessing: "O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us."

[^724]: Following the third section of Great Compline, the priest imparts the blessing: "God be bountiful unto us."

[^725]: A concluding colophon note in the Striatyn edition: *Коне́цъ повъседне́вной слꙋ́жбѣ* ("End of the Daily Service").

[^726]: An ornate Renaissance woodcut headpiece depicting the Crucifixion of Christ with the Mother of God and Saint John the Theologian, bearing the inscription *І Н Ц І* (Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews), introducing the collection of Daily Dismissals (*Ѿпꙋ́сты повъседне́вныѧ*).

[^727]: Liturgical dismissals in the Byzantine-Slavic tradition commemorate specific heavenly protectors on each day of the liturgical week (the weekly cycle begins on Sunday evening).

[^728]: The Monday dismissal commemorates the Archangels and the Bodiless Heavenly Powers (*застꙋпле́нїемъ чт҃ны́хъ нбⷵны́хъ си́лъ беспло́тныхъ*), to whom Monday is consecrated in the Octoechos.

[^729]: The liturgical day of Tuesday commences on Monday evening and continues through Tuesday Matins and the First Hour.

[^730]: The Tuesday dismissal commemorates the Holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (*чт҃на́гѡ и҆ сла́вна прⷪ҇рка, прⷣтча, и҆ крⷭ҇тлѧ і҆ѡа́нна*), the patron of repentance and monasticism.

[^731]: The liturgical day of Wednesday commences on Tuesday evening and continues through Wednesday Matins and the First Hour.

[^732]: The Wednesday dismissal commemorates the Precious and Life-Creating Cross of Christ (*си́лою чт҃на́го и҆ животворѧ́щаго крⷭ҇та*), commemorating the betrayal of the Lord by Judas.

[^733]: The Striatyn rubric notes that this same Cross-focused dismissal is repeated on Friday (*Се́йже и҆ въ па̑* / *пяток*), which commemorates the Crucifixion and Passion of Christ.

[^734]: The liturgical day of Thursday commences on Wednesday evening and continues through Thursday Matins and the First Hour.

[^735]: The Thursday dismissal commemorates the Holy Apostles (*ст҃ы́хъ сла́вны и҆ въсехва́лны а҆пл҃ъ*) and Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker (customarily added in general usage).

[^736]: The liturgical day of Friday commences on Thursday evening and continues through Friday Matins and the First Hour.

[^737]: The liturgical day of Saturday commences on Friday evening and continues through Saturday Matins and the Hours.

[^738]: Saturday in the Byzantine cursus is dedicated to All Saints and the commemoration of the faithful departed, combining intercessions for the Cross, Bodiless Powers, the Forerunner, Apostles, and Holy Martyrs (*ст҃ы́хъ и҆ добропобѣ́дныхъ мꙋ́ченикъ*).

[^739]: The Saturday dismissal explicitly includes the venerable God-bearing monastic fathers, the holy Ancestors of God Joachim and Anna, and the patron saint of the local church (*и҆ ст҃го і҆́мⷶ є҆го́же є҆́сть хра́мъ*).

[^740]: The rubric prescribes a concise common dismissal formula for the minor daily offices: the Ninth Hour, Compline, and the Midnight Office.

[^741]: The abbreviated penitential dismissal invoking Christ our true God through the prayers of the Theotokos and our venerable God-bearing fathers.

[^742]: An ornate Renaissance woodcut headpiece depicting Saint John Chrysostom in episcopal vestments holding the Holy Gospel, introducing the collection of Dismissals for the Great Feasts of the Lord, the Mother of God, and the Great Saints (*Ѿпꙋ́сты на пра́зникы влⷣчня и҆ прч҃тыѧ бц҃а...*).

[^743]: September 1 marks the Beginning of the Indiction (the ecclesiastical New Year in the Byzantine calendar) and the commemoration of Saint Symeon the Stylite (*прпⷣбнаго и҆ бг҃оноснаго ѻ҃ца нашего сѷмеѡ́на стлъ́пника*).

[^744]: The dismissal for September 1 combines the commemoration of the Mother of God with Saint Symeon the Stylite.

[^745]: September 8: The Great Feast of the Nativity of the Most Holy Theotokos (*Рожество̀ прч҃тыѧ бц҃а*), the first Great Feast of the liturgical year.

[^746]: The festal dismissal for the Nativity of the Theotokos includes the specific commemoration of her birth: *и҆ чт҃на́го є҆ѧ̀ рожества́* ("and by her precious Nativity").

[^747]: September 14: The Great Feast of the Universal Exaltation of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross (*Въздви́женїе чт҃на́ крⷭ҇та*).

[^748]: The festal dismissal for the Exaltation invokes the power of the precious and life-creating Cross: *си́лою чт҃наго и҆ животворѧ́щаго крⷭ҇та̀*.

[^749]: September 26: The commemoration of the Falling Asleep of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist John the Theologian (*Ст҃мꙋ а҆пл҃оу и҆ є҆ѵлї́стꙋ і҆ѡа́нноу бг҃осло́вꙋ*).

[^750]: The dismissal for Saint John the Theologian employs ornate baroque and traditional Byzantine epithets: "the Virgin" (*дѣ́твенника* / παρθένος), "who reclined upon His breast" (*напрѣ́стника* / ἐπιστήθιος, referring to John 13:25), and "the beloved disciple" (*възлю́бленнаго ᲂу҆́ченика*).

[^751]: November 21: The Great Feast of the Entry of the Most Holy Theotokos into the Temple (*Въвведе́нїе прч҃тыѧ бц҃а*), with its festal dismissal commemoration *и҆ чт҃на́гѡ є҆ѧ̀ въведе́нїа* ("and by her precious Entry").

[^752]: December 25: The Great Feast of the Nativity according to the Flesh of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ (*Рожество̀ Хрїсто́во*).

[^753]: The festal dismissal incipit for the Nativity of Christ: *Роже́йсѧ въ виѳлее́мѣ і҆ꙋде́йстѣмъ, и҆ въ ꙗ҆́слехъ възле́гый* ("May He Who was born in Bethlehem of Judea and lay in a manger...").

[^754]: January 1: The Feast of the Circumcision of our Lord Jesus Christ (*Ѡ҆брѣ́занїе Госпо́дне*) and the commemoration of Saint Basil the Great, Archbishop of Caesarea in Cappadocia.

[^755]: The festal dismissal incipit for the Circumcision: *Прїе́мый по зако́ноу ѡ҆смодне́вное ѡ҆брѣ́занїе* ("May He Who according to the Law accepted the eighth-day circumcision...").

[^756]: The dismissal for January 1 concludes by commemorating Saint Basil the Great, whose Divine Liturgy is celebrated on this day.

[^757]: At the Theophany of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ (January 6). Feast of the Holy Theophany and Baptism of Christ in the Jordan River.

[^758]: The festal incipit characteristic of Theophany: "May He Who deigned to be baptized by John in the Jordan for our salvation..." (Крестивыйся от Иоанна во Иордане...).

[^759]: The rubric abbreviation `по:` in Church Slavonic texts stands for the standard dismissal conclusion: `поми́луетъ и҆ спасе́тъ на́съ, ꙗ҆́ко бл҃гъ и҆ чл҃колꙋ́бецъ` ("have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind").

[^760]: At the Meeting (Hypapante / Presentation) of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ in the Temple (February 2), forty days after His Nativity.

[^761]: Festal dismissal incipit referencing the righteous elder Symeon receiving the infant Christ into his arms in the Temple (Luke 2:28): "Възлегий на руку праведнаго Симеона..."

[^762]: At the Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary by the Archangel Gabriel (March 25).

[^763]: On Lazarus Saturday, the eve of Palm Sunday, commemorating the raising from the dead of the righteous Lazarus after four days in the tomb (John 11).

[^764]: Lazarus Saturday festal dismissal incipit: "Пришедый в Вифанию, воздвигнути праведнаго Лазаря..."

[^765]: On Palm Sunday (Entry of the Lord into Jerusalem; Недѣля Цвѣтная / Вход Господень во Иерусалим). The dismissal text continues onto the following leaf.

[^766]: Palm Sunday festal dismissal incipit: "Пришедый во Иерусалим, и хвалу от детей еврейских восприемый..." (Matthew 21:8–16).

[^767]: Dismissal formula used during Holy and Great Week, specifically from Great Monday through Great Wednesday and at the appointed hours of the Great Week offices: "Пришедый на страсть вольную..." ("Who came unto His voluntary Passion...").

[^768]: Voluntary Passion of Christ: standard liturgical designation emphasizing the voluntary and redemptive nature of the Lord's suffering and kenotic sacrifice for the salvation of the world.

[^769]: On Holy and Great Saturday, commemorating the bodily burial of our Lord Jesus Christ, His rest in the tomb, and His descent into Hades to deliver the souls of the righteous.

[^770]: Holy Saturday festal dismissal incipit: "Иже нас ради волею положивыйся во гробе, и вшедый во ад..."

[^771]: On the Holy and Radiant Sunday of Pascha (Easter Sunday), the Feast of Feasts and Solemnity of Solemnities.

[^772]: The "Second Ektenia" refers to the Little Ektenia intoned after the introductory Paschal stichoi and the Great Ektenia at the beginning of Paschal Matins.

[^773]: Paschal exclamation: "Яко подобает Тебе всякая слава, честь и поклонение, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^774]: Universal liturgical rubric for the Paschal Canon composed by Saint John of Damascus: throughout the entire Bright Week (Светлая седмица), after each ode of the canon, a Little Ektenia (малая ектения) is intoned either by the deacon or, in the absence of a deacon, by the priest, followed by its appointed festive exclamation.

[^775]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 1 of the Paschal Canon: "Яко Твоя держава, и Твое есть царство, и сила, и слава, Отца, и Сына, и Святаго Духа, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^776]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 3 of the Paschal Canon: "Яко Ты еси Бог наш, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^777]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 4 of the Paschal Canon: "Яко благ и человеколюбец Бог еси, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^778]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 5 of the Paschal Canon: "Яко благословися и прославися всечестное и великолепое имя Твое, Отца и Сына и Святаго Духа, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^779]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 6 of the Paschal Canon: "Ты бо еси Царь мира и Спас душ наших, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^780]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 7 of the Paschal Canon: "Буди держава царствия Твоего благословена и препрославлена, Отца и Сына и Святаго Духа..." (witnessed here by the recensional incipit `Яко святися и прославися...`).

[^781]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 8 of the Paschal Canon: "Яко Ты еси Бог наш, и Тебе славу возсылаем..." (or "Яко Отец щедрот и Бог всякаго утешения...").

[^782]: Priestly exclamation after Ode 9 of the Paschal Canon: "Яко Тебе хвалят вся силы небесныя, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь."

[^783]: The festive dismissal used at all divine services throughout Bright Week, uniquely commenced by the chanting of the Paschal Troparion as the dismissal incipit.

[^784]: The Paschal Troparion ("Christ is risen from the dead...") sung directly by the celebrant as the opening proclamation of the dismissal.

[^785]: On Thomas Sunday (Antipascha / Second Sunday of Pascha), celebrating the eighth day of the Resurrection and the manifestation of the risen Christ to Saint Thomas (John 20:19–29).

[^786]: Thomas Sunday festal dismissal incipit: "Пришедый ко учеником, и дверем затвореном, и Фому уверивый..."

[^787]: At the Ascension of our Lord Jesus Christ, forty days after His Resurrection (Acts 1:9–11).

[^788]: Ascension festal dismissal incipit: "Вознесыйся во славе от нас на небо, и седый одесную Бога и Отца..."

[^789]: On the Sunday of Holy Pentecost (Trinity Sunday), fifty days after Pascha, commemorating the descent of the Holy Spirit upon the holy Apostles in the form of fiery tongues (Acts 2).

[^790]: Pentecost festal dismissal incipit: "Низпославый Пресвятый Свой Дух на святыя Своя ученики и апостолы..."

[^791]: At the Holy Transfiguration of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ upon Mount Tabor (August 6; Matthew 17:1–9).

[^792]: Transfiguration festal dismissal incipit: "Преобразивыйся на горе Фаворстей, показа учеником славу Свою..."

[^793]: At the Dormition (Falling Asleep) of our Most Holy Lady Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary (August 15).

[^794]: Canonical rubrical rule governing the Feasts of the Master (Господские праздники) and of the Mother of God (Богородичные праздники): the appointed festal dismissal is intoned daily from the feast day itself through the Apodosis (отдание / apodosis) of the celebration.

[^795]: Daily dismissal for a saint who has a Great Doxology (Славословие великое) appointed at Matins, indicating a saint of Polyeleos or Doxology rank under the Typikon.

[^796]: Order and Prayer for the Blessing of Kolyvo (boiled wheat mixed with honey and dried fruits) offered in honor and commemoration of a saint, traditionally celebrated on the patronal feast or annual memorial of a martyr or holy father.

[^797]: The liturgical positioning of the blessing of kolyvo at the Divine Liturgy occurs immediately following the triple singing of "Blessed be the Name of the Lord" (Буди имя Господне благословено отныне и до века) after the Prayer behind the Ambon.

[^798]: While the choir chants the troparion of the commemorated saint, the priest or deacon censes the kolyvo arranged upon the tetrapod in the midst of the nave.

[^799]: Opening address of the Prayer over Kolyvo for a Saint, invoking God's creation of the cosmos through His divine Logos (Psalm 32:6 / 33:6; John 1:3).

[^800]: Biblical allusion to the Three Holy Youths (Ananias, Azarias, and Misael) and the Prophet Daniel at the royal court in Babylon, who abstained from the defiling meat and wine of King Nebuchadnezzar's table and flourished on pulse (seeds) and water (Daniel 1:12–16).

[^801]: The blessing of the boiled seeds (wheat, barley, raisins, nuts) signifies both bodily resurrection from the earth (John 12:24; 1 Corinthians 15:36–38) and the sweetness of the kingdom of heaven.

[^802]: Liturgical intention formula: the offering of the kolyvo is dedicated to the glory of God and the veneration of the saint, as well as for the spiritual benefit of the faithful who adorned the church and reposed in true piety.

[^803]: Slavonic `благоукрасивши сия` (Greek: τῶν καλλωπισάντων ταῦτα) denotes the faithful donors and servants who prepared, adorned, and presented the dish of kolyvo for the holy rite.

[^804]: Order of the Memorial Litany (Заупокойная ектения) and Prayer of Absolution over Kolyvo offered for the deceased (Kolyvo za usopshikh / Panakhyda / Parastas).

[^805]: The abbreviation `з҃•` in the Slavonic rubric stands for `зри́` ("Note!" / "Behold!"), directing that the appointed funeral troparia are chanted immediately following the triple "Blessed be the Name of the Lord".

[^806]: The venerable troparion of the Byzantine memorial office (Tone 4): "Со духи праведных скончавшихся, душу раба Твоего, Спасе, упокой..." ("With the spirits of the righteous departed, give rest to the soul of Thy servant, O Savior...").

[^807]: Contemporary 17th-century semi-uncial marginal note `поминати` ("to commemorate"), reminding the celebrating deacon or priest to insert the proper names of the departed at this diaconal petition.

[^808]: Opening petition of the Memorial Ektenia, citing LXX Psalm 50:1 ("Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy").

[^809]: Traditional liturgical formula for the departed: `блаженныя памяти и оставления грехов преже почившему...`

[^810]: The priest recites the ancient and venerable Byzantine Prayer for the Departed ("Боже духов и всякия плоти...") secretly while the deacon intones the memorial petitions.

[^811]: Direct quotation from Isaiah 35:10 / 51:11 ("sorrow and sighing shall flee away"), evocative of the peaceful repose of the soul in Abraham's bosom.

[^812]: Scriptural citations: Ecclesiastes 7:20 ("For there is not a just man upon earth, that does good, and sins not") and LXX Psalm 118:142 ("Thy righteousness is an everlasting righteousness, and Thy law is the truth").

[^813]: Priestly exclamation of the Memorial Prayer: "Яко Ты еси воскресение, и живот, и упокой усопшаго раба Твоего..."

[^814]: Typographical colophon marking the formal conclusion (`КОНЕЦЪ`) of the Dismissals, Benedictions, and Kolyvo section of the 1604 Striatyn Liturgikon.

[^815]: Physical lacuna in the unique Striatyn exemplar: leaf 2 of Quire Ya (`ѧ҃ в`, corresponding to unpreserved folios 269bis r-v / pp. 527–528) is wanting in this binding. It contained the general title and headpiece for the Evening and Morning Offices, the introductory rubrics for the Priest at Vespers, and the opening petition of the First Prayer of the Lucernarium ("Господи щедрый и милостивый..."). The text resumes on folio 3 recto (`ѧ҃ г`, misnumbered 524 for 527) with the final doxological exclamation of the First Prayer.

[^816]: The Seven Prayers of Light (Светильничные молитвы / Prayers of the Lucernarium) recited secretly by the priest during the reading of the Introductory Psalm (Psalm 103) at Byzantine Vespers.

[^817]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the First Prayer of Light: "Яко подобает Тебе всякая слава, честь и поклонение, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно..."

[^818]: The Second Prayer of Light recited by the priest at Vespers ("Господи, не яростию Твоею обличи нас...").

[^819]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 6:1 / Psalm 37:1 ("O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine indignation, neither chasten me in Thy wrath").

[^820]: The Third Prayer of Light recited by the priest at Vespers ("Господи Боже наш, помяни нас грешных и непотребных раб Твоих...").

[^821]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Third Prayer of Light: "Яко благий человеколюбец Бог еси, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно [и во веки веков. Аминь]".

[^822]: The Fourth Prayer of Light at Vespers (`Молитва, д҃`: "Иже немалчными песньми, и непрестанными славословльми..."), corresponding to the Greek Εὐχὴ δʹ: Ὁ ἀσιγήτοις ὕμνοις καὶ ἀπαύστοις δοξολογίαις...

[^823]: Doxological exclamation of the Fourth Prayer of Light (`Въглаше́: Яко подобает ти всяка слава, честь:`), corresponding to the Greek Ὅτι πρέπει σοι πᾶσα δόξα, τιμὴ καὶ προσκύνησις...

[^824]: The Fifth Prayer of Light at Vespers (`Молитва, пятая`: "Благословен еси Господи Боже вседержителю, сведый ум человечь..."), corresponding to the Greek Εὐχὴ εʹ: Εὐλογητὸς εἶ, Κύριε, ὁ Θεὸς ὁ Παντοκράτωρ, ὁ εἰδὼς τὸν νοῦν τὸν ἀνθρώπινον...

[^825]: Biblical allusion to Ephesians 3:20 ("Now unto Him that is able to do exceeding abundantly above all that we ask or think").

[^826]: Scriptural formula citing LXX Psalm 50:1 / Psalm 68:17 ("according to the multitude of Thy tender mercies").

[^827]: Direct petitions from the Lord's Prayer (Matthew 6:13: "And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil").

[^828]: The Sixth Prayer of Light at Vespers (`Молитва, ѕ҃`: "Господи, Господи, иже пречистою ти силою приемляй всячьская..."), corresponding to the Greek Εὐχὴ Ϛʹ: Κύριε, Κύριε, ὁ τῇ ἀχράντῳ σου δυνάμει περιέχων τὰ σύμπαντα...

[^829]: The Seventh Prayer of Light at Vespers (`Молитва, з҃`: "Боже великий и дивный, иже неизреченною благостию и богатым промыслом..."), corresponding to the Greek Εὐχὴ ζʹ: Ὁ Θεὸς ὁ μέγας καὶ θαυμαστός, ὁ ἀνεκδιηγήτῳ ἀγαθωσύνῃ καὶ πλουσίᾳ προνοίᾳ διοικῶν τὰ ἀνθρώπινα... The prayer is split typographically across the misbound leaf `ѧ҃ в` (pp. 527–528), opening here on fol. 273v (p. 536) and resuming on fol. 274r (p. 537).

[^830]: Cinnabar title in tall decorative Cyrillic ligature typography (`МОЛИТВЫ ВЕЧЕРНЇА`) preceded by an elaborate Renaissance foliate woodcut headband with central floral urn, inaugurating the Evening Prayers (Prayers of Light / Prayers of the Lucernarium) at Vespers.

[^831]: Important codicological finding in the Striatyn exemplar: leaf 2 of Quire Ya (`ѧ҃ в`, pp. 527–528), which in Cohort 27 was noted as missing between fol. 269v (p. 526) and fol. 270r (p. [527 recte 529]), was in fact misbound by the 17th-century binder into the middle of the quire between fol. 273v (p. 536) and fol. 274r (p. 537). Its recto (p. 527) contains the formal headpiece, the section title `МОЛИТВЫ ВЕЧЕРНЇА`, and the first half of the First Prayer of Light; its verso (p. 528) contains the second half of the First Prayer of Light down to the rubric `Възглаше́нїе`, whose exclamation was printed on fol. 270r (`ѧ҃ г`, Cohort 27). The Seventh Prayer of Light, begun on fol. 273v, continues directly onto fol. 274r (p. 537).

[^832]: The First Prayer of Light at Vespers (`Молитва, первая`: "Господи щедрый и милостивый, долготерпеливе и многомилостиве..."), corresponding to the Greek Εὐχὴ αʹ: Κύριε οἰκτίρμων καὶ ἐλεήμων, μακρόθυμε καὶ πολυέλεε...

[^833]: Scriptural invocation from Exodus 34:6 and LXX Psalm 102:8 ("The Lord is compassionate and merciful, long-suffering and plenteous in mercy").

[^834]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 85:6 ("Give ear, O Lord, unto my prayer; and attend to the voice of my supplication").

[^835]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 85:17 ("Work with me a sign for good").

[^836]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 85:11 ("Guide me, O Lord, in Thy way, and I will walk in Thy truth").

[^837]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 85:11 ("Let my heart rejoice, that I may fear Thy name").

[^838]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 85:10 ("For Thou art great, and workest wonders: Thou art God alone").

[^839]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 85:8 ("There is none like unto Thee among the gods, O Lord").

[^840]: Textual restoration of the lower margin of fol. 528, which suffered physical paper fraying: `[вся уповающия на тя... Святое Имя Твое]`, corresponding to Greek εἰς τὸ βοηθεῖν καὶ παρακαλεῖν καὶ σῴζειν πάντας τοὺς ἐλπίζοντας εἰς τὸ ὄνομά σου τὸ ἅγιον.

[^841]: The priestly exclamation for this First Prayer of Light (`Яко подобает ти всяка слава, честь, и поклонение...`) is inked on folio 270r (`ѧ҃ г`, p. [529], Cohort 27), exactly where the original quire text resumes after this misbound leaf.

[^842]: The Eighth Prayer of Light at Vespers (`Молитва, осмая`: "Боже великий и дивный, един имея безсмертие..."), corresponding to the ancient Byzantine Prayer of the Bowing of Heads at Vespers (Εὐχὴ τῆς κεφαλοκλισίας) or Eighth Lucernarium Prayer: Ὁ Θεὸς ὁ μέγας καὶ ὕψιστος, ὁ μόνος ἔχων ἀθανασίαν... In medieval and early modern Slavic liturgika, the seven secret prayers and the prayer of the bowing of heads were frequently numbered consecutively as eight prayers.

[^843]: Direct quotation from 1 Timothy 6:16 ("Who only hath immortality").

[^844]: Direct quotation from 1 Timothy 6:16 ("dwelling in the light which no man can approach unto").

[^845]: Scriptural allusion to LXX Psalm 103:24 ("In wisdom hast Thou made them all").

[^846]: Creation narrative citation from Genesis 1:4 ("and God divided the light from the darkness").

[^847]: Biblical citation of Genesis 1:16–18 and LXX Psalm 135:8–9 ("The sun to rule over the day... the moon and stars to rule over the night").

[^848]: Scriptural quotation from LXX Psalm 94:2 ("Let us come before His presence with thanksgiving").

[^849]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 140:2 ("Let my prayer be set forth before Thee as incense").

[^850]: Sacrificial phrase evocative of Genesis 8:21 and Ephesians 5:2 ("an offering and a sacrifice to God for a sweetsmelling savor").

[^851]: Pauline exhortation from Romans 13:12 ("let us put on the armor of light").

[^852]: Direct citations from the protection Psalm (LXX Psalm 90:5–6: "Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night; nor for the arrow that flies by day; nor for the pestilence that walks in darkness").

[^853]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Eighth Prayer of Light, marking the liturgical termination of the Evening Prayers of Vespers.

[^854]: General title in tall red cinnabar Cyrillic ligatures (`МОЛИТВЫ ОУТРЕНЇА`) beneath an elaborate Renaissance woodcut headpiece depicting Christ as the Man of Sorrows (Христос во гробе / "Царь Славы"), standing in the open sepulchre before the Cross, flanked by rich floral strapwork. This begins the series of Twelve Morning Prayers recited secretly by the priest during the reading of the Hexapsalmos at Matins.

[^855]: The First Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, первая`: "Благодарим Тя, Господи Боже наш, воздвигшаго ны от лож наших..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ αʹ: Εὐχαριστοῦμέν σοι, Κύριε ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, τῷ ἐξεγείραντι ἡμᾶς ἐκ τῶν κοιτῶν ἡμῶν...

[^856]: Doxological exclamation of the First Morning Prayer (`Възглашение: Яко подобает ти всяка слава, честь, и поклонение...`), corresponding to Greek Ὅти πρέπει σοι πᾶσα δόξα, τιμὴ καὶ προσκύνησις...

[^857]: The Second Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, в҃`: "От нощи утренюет дух мой к Тебе, Боже..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ βʹ: Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει τὸ πνεῦμά μου πρὸς σέ, ὁ Θεός...

[^858]: Direct quotation of the biblical canticle incipit from Isaiah 26:9 ("With my soul have I desired Thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I seek Thee early: for when Thy judgments are in the earth, the inhabitants of the world will learn righteousness").

[^859]: Scriptural quotation from 2 Corinthians 7:1 ("perfecting holiness in the fear of God").

[^860]: Liturgical petition citing LXX Psalm 27:9 ("Save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance").

[^861]: Concluding exclamation of the Second Morning Prayer (`Възглашение: Яко благословися и прославися пречестное и великолепое имя Твое...`), corresponding to Greek Ὅти ηὐλόγηται καὶ δεδόξασται τὸ πάνтимон καὶ μεγαλοπρεπὲς ὄνομά σου...

[^862]: The Third Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, г҃`: "От нощи утренюет дух наш к Тебе, Боже..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ γʹ: Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει τὸ πνεῦμα ἡμῶν πρὸς σέ, ὁ Θεός...

[^863]: Direct quotation of Isaiah 26:9 ("From night our spirit awakens early unto Thee, O God...").

[^864]: Citing LXX Psalm 118:12, 26, 68 ("teach me Thy statutes").

[^865]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 12:4 ("Lighten mine eyes, lest I sleep the sleep of death").

[^866]: Messianic title from Malachi 4:2 ("the Sun of righteousness").

[^867]: Biblical quotation from the Canticle of Zechariah (Luke 1:79: "to guide our feet into the way of peace").

[^868]: Priestly exclamation of the Third Morning Prayer (`Възглашение: Яко Твоя держава, и Твое есть царство...`), corresponding to Greek Ὅти σὸν τὸ κράτος, καὶ σοῦ ἐστιν ἡ βασιλεία καὶ ἡ δύναμις καὶ ἡ δόξα...

[^869]: Typographical error in the 1604 Striatyn print: `цр҃тко` for `цр҃тво` (царство / "kingdom").

[^870]: The Fourth Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, четвертая`: "Владыко Боже святый и непостижимый..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ δʹ: Δέσποτα Θεέ, ἅγιε καὶ ἀκατάληπτε, ὁ εἰπὼν ἐκ σκότους φῶς λάμψαι...

[^871]: Direct quotation of 2 Corinthians 4:6 ("For God, Who commanded the light to shine out of darkness, hath shined in our hearts").

[^872]: Biblical allusion to 1 Thessalonians 5:5 ("Ye are all the children of light, and the children of the day").

[^873]: Citing Romans 8:17 and Galatians 4:7 on inheritance of the divine promises.

[^874]: Scriptural formula citing LXX Psalm 50:1 and Psalm 68:17 ("according to the multitude of Thy tender mercies").

[^875]: Scriptural allusion to LXX Psalm 102:22 ("Bless the Lord, all His works in all places of His dominion").

[^876]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Fourth Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: Яко Бог милостем и щедротам, и человеколюбец еси, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне [и присно, и во веки веков. Аминь]`, corresponding to Greek Ὅτι Θεὸς ἐλέους, οἰκτιρμῶν καὶ φιλανθρωπίας ὑπάρχεις...

[^877]: The Fifth Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, є҃`: "Благих сокровище, источниче приснотекущий..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ εʹ: Θησαυρὲ τῶν ἀγαθῶν, πηγὴ ἀέναος, Πάτερ ἅγιε θαυματουργέ...

[^878]: Typographical corruption in the 1604 Striatyn print: `оу҆́чстии` for `ѿч҃е ст҃ый` (Πάτερ ἅγιε / "holy Father"), where the compositor misread the manuscript ligature `ѿч҃` + `ст҃` as `оучстии`.

[^879]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 140:2 ("Let my prayer be set forth before Thee as incense").

[^880]: Scriptural allusion to 2 Corinthians 13:5–7 on being found reprobate; Greek: καὶ μηδένα ἡμῶν ἀδόκιμον ποιήσῃς; Slavonic: `да ни единого же на[съ] неискусна сътвориши`.

[^881]: Ancient Byzantine liturgical formula: εἰς τὸ ὑπερουράνιον καὶ νοερόν σου θυσιαστήριον (`въ пренебныи и мысльныи твои жрътовникъ`).

[^882]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Fifth Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: Яко ты еси Бог наш, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу, ныне и присно и во веки [веков. Аминь]`, corresponding to Greek Ὅτι σὺ εἶ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν...

[^883]: The Sixth Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, шестая`: "Благодарим Тя, Господи Боже спасений наших..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ Ϛʹ: Εὐχαριστοῦμέν σοι, Κύριε ὁ Θεὸς τῶν σωτηρίων ἡμῶν...

[^884]: Greek: ἐν τῷ παρελθόντι τῆς νυκτὸς μέτρῳ; Slavonic: `въ мимошедшее нощи число`.

[^885]: Biblical allusion to LXX Psalm 46:8 ("sing ye praises with understanding").

[^886]: Apostolic injunction from 1 Thessalonians 5:17 ("Pray without ceasing").

[^887]: Direct quotation of Philippians 2:12 ("work out your own salvation with fear and trembling").

[^888]: Greek: καὶ σύντριψον ὑπὸ τοὺς πόδας αὐτῶν τοὺς ἀοράτους καὶ πολεμίους ἐχθρούς. The 1604 Striatyn text reads `неѡрителны [неѡрателны] врагъ` for `ратныя враги` (warring / hostile enemies).

[^889]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Sixth Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: Яко ты еси царь мира, и спас душам нашим, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу...`, corresponding to Greek Ὅτι σὺ εἶ ὁ βασιλεὺς τῆς εἰρήνης καὶ σωτὴρ τῶν ψυχῶν ἡμῶν...

[^890]: The Seventh Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, седмая`: "Боже и Отче Господа нашего Иисуса Христа..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ ζʹ: Ὁ Θεὸς καὶ Πατὴρ τοῦ Κυρίου ἡμῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ...

[^891]: Scriptural address citing 2 Corinthians 1:3 and Ephesians 1:3 ("Blessed be the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ").

[^892]: Biblical allusion to LXX Psalm 50:17 ("O Lord, open Thou my lips") and Ephesians 6:19 ("that utterance may be given unto me, that I may open my mouth boldly").

[^893]: Direct quotation from LXX Psalm 118:12, 26, 68 ("teach me Thy statutes").

[^894]: Direct citation and theological paraphrase of Romans 8:26 ("For we know not what we should pray for as we ought: but the Spirit itself makes intercession for us").

[^895]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 129:3–4 ("If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand? For with Thee is forgiveness [propitiation]").

[^896]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Seventh Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: Буди держава царствия Твоего благословенна и препрославленна, Отца и Сына и Святаго Духа...`, corresponding to Greek Εἴη τὸ κράτος τῆς βασιλείας σου εὐλογημένον...

[^897]: The Eighth Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, и҃`: "Господи Боже наш, иже сонное уныние отложивый [отгнавый] от нас..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ ηʹ: Κύριε ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, ὁ τὴν τοῦ ὕπνου ῥᾳθυμίαν ἀποσκεδάσας ἀφ' ἡμῶν...

[^898]: Greek: ὁ τὴν τοῦ ὕπνου ῥᾳθυμίαν ἀποσκεδάσας ἀφ' ἡμῶν ("Who hast scattered from us the slothfulness of sleep").

[^899]: Scriptural quotation from 2 Timothy 1:9 ("Who hath saved us, and called us with a holy calling").

[^900]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 133:2 ("Lift up your hands in the sanctuary, and bless the Lord").

[^901]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 118:62 ("At midnight I rose to give thanks unto Thee because of the judgments of Thy righteousness").

[^902]: Pauline triad of virtues: faith unashamed (Romans 1:16, 2 Timothy 1:12), steadfast hope (Hebrews 6:19), and love unfeigned (Romans 12:9, 2 Corinthians 6:6, 1 Peter 1:22).

[^903]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 120:8 ("The Lord shall preserve thy coming in and thy going out from this time forth, and even for evermore").

[^904]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Eighth Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: Яко благословися и прославися пречестное и великолепое имя Твое, Отца и Сына и Святаго Духа...`, corresponding to Greek Ὅτι ηὐλόγηται καὶ δεδόξασται τὸ πάνтимон καὶ μεγαλοπρεπὲς ὄνομά σου...

[^905]: The Ninth Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, девятая`: "Господи Боже наш, иже покаяния ради оставление человеком даровав..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ θʹ: Κύριε ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, ὁ διὰ μετανοίας ἄφεσιν τοῖς ἀνθρώποις χαρισάμενος...

[^906]: Scriptural reference to the repentance of King David following his rebuke by Nathan the Prophet (2 Samuel 12:13; LXX Psalm 50).

[^907]: Direct quotation of the opening verses of the Great Penitential Psalm (LXX Psalm 50:1–2: "Have mercy upon me, O God, according to Thy great mercy: according unto the multitude of Thy tender mercies blot out my transgressions").

[^908]: Scriptural allusions to LXX Psalm 43:22 ("for He knows the secrets of the heart") and LXX Psalm 50:8 ("the hidden and secret things of Thy wisdom").

[^909]: Biblical declaration of divine prerogative to remit sins (Mark 2:7, Luke 5:21: "Who can forgive sins but God only?").

[^910]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 50:12, 14 ("Create in me a clean heart, O God... and uphold me with Thy free [governing] Spirit").

[^911]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 50:14 ("Restore unto me the joy of Thy salvation").

[^912]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 50:13 ("Cast me not away from Thy presence; and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me").

[^913]: Direct citation of LXX Psalm 50:21 ("Then shalt Thou be pleased with the sacrifice of righteousness, with burnt offering and whole burnt offering: then shall they offer bullocks upon Thine altar").

[^914]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Ninth Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: [Милостию и щедротами, и человеколюбием] Единороднаго Сына Твоего, с нимже благословен еси...`, corresponding to Greek Ἐλέει καὶ οἰκтирμοῖς καὶ φιλανθρωπίᾳ τοῦ μονογενοῦς σου Υἱοῦ...

[^915]: The Tenth Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, десятая`: "Боже, Боже наш, иже умныя и словесныя составивый силы..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ ιʹ: Ὁ Θεὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, ὁ τὰς νοερὰς καὶ λογικὰς συστησάμενος δυνάμεις τῷ σῷ θελήματι...

[^916]: Patristic definition of the bodiless heavenly hierarchies as noetic and rational powers created by the divine will.

[^917]: Direct quotation of Philippians 2:10 ("that at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth").

[^918]: Scriptural quotation of LXX Psalm 150:6 ("Let every thing that hath breath praise the Lord").

[^919]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Tenth Morning Prayer: `Възглашение: Яко Тя хвалят вся силы небесныя, и Тебе славу возсылаем, Отцу и Сыну и Святому Духу...`, corresponding to Greek Ὅτι σὲ αἰνοῦσι πᾶσαι αἱ δυνάμεις τῶν οὐρανῶν...

[^920]: The Eleventh Morning Prayer at Matins (`Молитва, а҃і`: "Хвалим, и поем, благословим и благодарим Тя, Боже отец наших..."), corresponding to Greek Εὐχὴ ιαʹ: Αἰνοῦμεν, ὑμνοῦμεν, εὐλογοῦμεν καὶ εὐχαριστοῦμέν σοι, ὁ Θεὸς τῶν πατέρων ἡμῶν...

[^921]: Scriptural invocation citing Daniel 3:26, 52 (Song of the Three Holy Children: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers").

[^922]: Messianic title from Malachi 4:2 ("the Sun of righteousness").

[^923]: Direct quotation of Romans 13:13 ("Let us walk honestly [becomingly], as in the day").

[^924]: Biblical quotation from LXX Psalm 118:32 ("I will run the way of Thy commandments").

[^925]: Direct quotation of LXX Psalm 35:10 ("For with Thee is the fountain of life: in Thy light shall we see light").

[^926]: Scriptural allusion to 1 Timothy 6:16 ("dwelling in the light which no man can approach unto").

[^927]: Concluding doxological exclamation of the Matins Prayers: `Възглашение: [Яко Твоя держава, и Твое есть] царство, и сила, и слава, Отца, и Сына, и Святаго Духа, ныне, и присно, и в веки веком. Аминь`.

[^928]: Typographical error in the 1604 print: `цр҃тко` for `цр҃тво` (царство / "kingdom").

[^929]: Concluding inscription (`Конецъ, Молитвамъ`) marking the definitive close of the corpus of secret priestly prayers for Vespers and Matins in the Striatyn Liturgikon.

[^930]: Early modern technical phrase: `изображение книги сея` (the typographic printing / impression of this book).

[^931]: Bishop Hedeon Balaban (Hryhoriy Balaban, 1530–1607), Orthodox Bishop of Lviv, Halych, and Kamianets-Podilskyi from 1569 until his death in 1607, prominent defender of the Ruthenian Orthodox Church who opposed the Union of Brest (1596) and founded private printing presses at his family estates in Striatyn and Derman.

[^932]: Title of Exarch of the Ecumenical Patriarchate of Constantinople (`Е́ѯархъ въселенского прⷭ҇тла`), bestowed upon Bishop Hedeon Balaban in 1589 by Patriarch Jeremias II Tranos during his journey through the Ruthenian lands of the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth.

[^933]: Lord Teodor (Fedir) Balaban (d. 1606), nephew of Bishop Hedeon Balaban, Starosta of Terebovlia, wealthy Ruthenian nobleman and patron of letters who financed and housed the Striatyn press.

[^934]: Scriptural allusion to Philippians 3:13 ("reaching forth unto those things which are before").

[^935]: The editor/proofreader (`исправитель`), historically identified as Pamvo Berynda (c. 1550–1632), the renowned scholar, lexicographer, and monk-typographer who was invited by the Balabans to lead the editorial and printing operations at Striatyn.

[^936]: Traditional printer's apology (colophonic excusatio typographi), standard across Early Modern Cyrillic, Greek, and Latin incunabula and post-incunabula.

[^937]: Striatyn (Стрятин, today in Rohatyn Raion, Ivano-Frankivsk Oblast, Ukraine), ancestral estate of the Balaban family where Bishop Hedeon and Fedir established their typographic workshop in 1602.

[^938]: Byzantine Era of the World (Anno Mundi 7112 = 1604 AD, calculated as 7112 – 5508 = 1604). The printing of the Striatyn Liturgikon was completed on January 20, 1604.

[^939]: Slavonic: `Погрѣше́нїа` (Errata / Printing Corrigenda). An explicit corrigenda leaf was a distinctive feature of high-standard Renaissance humanistic and post-incunabula typography introduced to Ruthenian printing by Bishop Hedeon Balaban and his master printer Pamvo Berynda.

[^940]: Slavonic: `В ли́стѣ р҃а` (At leaf 101, or folio/page 101/104). Refers to the petitional suffrage in the Ektenia where the typesetter omitted the standard liturgical phrase `и стра́хомъ бж҃їимъ`.

[^941]: Slavonic: `бл҃гоговѣ́нїемъ въходѧ́щихъ кънъ` ("of those entering therein with reverence", where `кънъ` represents `къ нь` / `въ нь`, "into it").

[^942]: Slavonic: `ты чьтѝ` (read thou / imperative singular directed to the officiating priest or reader).

[^943]: Slavonic: `бл҃гоговѣ́нїемъ и стра́хомъ бж҃їимъ въходѧ́щихъ кънъ` ("of those who with reverence and the fear of God enter therein"). Restoring the canonical tripartite formula `съ вѣ́рою, бл҃гоговѣ́нїемъ и҆ стра́хомъ бж҃їимъ въходѧ́щихъ` of the Great Litany and Litany of Fervent Supplication.

[^944]: Slavonic: `к ли́стѣ с҃к҃д` (at leaf 224). Refers to the special petitional suffrage for rain during drought (`А҆́щеже бꙋ́детъ бездо́жїе`).

[^945]: Slavonic: `И҆ е́же ѡ҆дъжити дъжъ бл҃гъ` ("And that [He] may send down rain, good...").

[^946]: Slavonic: `тыже чьтѝ` (read thou likewise).

[^947]: Slavonic: `И҆ о́еже ѡ҆дъждити земли дъжъ бл҃гъ` ("And that He may send down rain upon the earth, good..."). The corrector restores the missing direct object `землѝ` ("upon the earth").

[^948]: Slavonic: `Прѡ́чаꙗ оу҆до́бнѣ разсмотрели́въ чита́тель испра́витъ` (Traditional colophonic and errata formula: "The rest, the attentive/discerning reader will easily correct", invoking the charitable indulgence and editorial vigilance of the clergy and reader).

[^949]: Archival accession stamps and inventory shelfmarks of the State Public Library of the Ukrainian SSR (now the Vernadsky National Library of Ukraine, Kyiv, Department of Early Printed Books and Rare Editions), where this exemplar of the 1604 Striatyn Liturgikon is preserved under shelfmark `7693 / СД-90` (formerly `ТКВ-4659`).

[^950]: Codicological description of the rear pastedown, demonstrating standard Late Renaissance and Early Modern Slavic binding structures: blind-tooled calf leather over bevelled oak or beech boards, laced onto four split-strap alum-tawed or tanned leather sewing supports.

[^951]: Exterior codicological description of the rear binding board of the 1604 Striatyn Liturgikon, completing the complete physical description of this rare monument of Ukrainian typography.
